more recent sample compared to the NNCS.
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=190) width=188.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Another concern with cross-sectional analysis is reverse causality. Speci?cally,
page=28 block=22427354 (x=12 y=202) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the relationship between gun shops and violent crime could operate in the oppo-
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=214) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
site direction from what we hypothesize. In this case, gun shops could be viewed
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=226) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as a reaction to homicides and/or robberies in a local area. Seeking self-
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=238) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
protection, residents of high-crime neighborhoods may demand or provide a
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=250) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
market for gun dealers. While it is impossible to rule this out of any cross-
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=262) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
sectional analysis, we provide conceptual and empirical evidence supporting our
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=274) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
argument that FFLs in?uence neighborhood violent crime, rather than crime
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=286) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
in?uencing the location of FFLs. Retail development often follows reductions in
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=298) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
crime (Stacy, Ho, and Pendall 2016), and high crime can stymie development in
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=310) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
urban areas, regardless of any stimulus programs that local governments may
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=322) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
put in place (Bowes 2007). It seems highly unlikely that FFLs would operate in a
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=334) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
different manner. Indeed, descriptive results presented in table 1 suggest that
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=346) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
crime rates are actually higher in neighborhoods without gun shops. To empiri-
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=358) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cally address the possibility of reverse causality, we rerun models on a subset of
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=370) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
recent data from the multi-city study of neighborhood crime (Baumer et al.
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=382) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
2014) and include a lagged indicator of robbery from the NNCS and present the
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=394) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
results in table S3. If FFLs were opening as a result of neighborhood crime rates,
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=406) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
then robberies in 2000 should completely explain the relationship between FFLs
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=418) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and robberies in 2006–2009. Although the coef?cient for our FFL variable is
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=430) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
noticeably smaller in table S3, it remains signi?cant and positive.
page=28 block=22427354 (x=1 y=442) width=274.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Discussion and Conclusion
page=28 block=10096765 (x=1 y=469) width=154.000000 height=9.000000 fontname=HOMJHN+AdvOT0a81dd96.B wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Existing research has suggested that local institutions can directly and indirectly
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=490) width=342.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in?uence crime rates. Routine activities theory suggests that institutions can
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=502) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
directly in?uence crime by providing suitable targets and by concentrating
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=514) width=342.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
potential victims, while social disorganization theory suggests that they can indi-
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=526) width=342.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
rectly in?uence crime by signaling disorganization in a neighborhood, which can
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=538) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
discourage social control efforts (Kubrin et al. 2011; Kubrin and Hipp 2014).
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=550) width=342.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
Our research adds to this discussion by considering federally licensed gun shops
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=562) width=342.000000 height=6.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
as neighborhood institutions. We argue that along with providing opportunities
page=28 block=22456922 (x=1 y=574) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 29 left:1 upper:1
292 Social Forces 96(1)
page=29 block=5046276 (x=1 y=1) width=77.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=HOMJHO+AdvOTb4959fff wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
for crime, gun shops could signal disorder and indirectly contribute to crime
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=34) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
rates. To test this relationship, we employ multilevel Poisson models using data
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=46) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
from the NNCS, FFLD, and NETS. Results from these models suggest that there
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=58) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
is a relationship between gun shop presence and local crime rates in urban areas.
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=70) width=341.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Speci?cally, our results indicate that homicide and robbery rates increase as
page=29 block=246564962 (x=22 y=82) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
the number of gun shops in a neighborhood increases (weighted by the tract
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=94) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
population). This relationship remains even after accounting for other factors
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=106) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
associated with elevated crime rates at both the tract and metropolitan level. It
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=118) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
also holds regardless of shop type, with one exception. Big box stores with gun
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=130) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
licenses are not associated with higher homicide rates, though they are associ-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=142) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ated with higher robbery rates. A possible explanation for this is that big box
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=154) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
stores have differential effects on homicides and robberies. In short, the presence
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=166) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of a department store or sporting goods store could provide suitable targets for
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=178) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
robbers, but not instill the same concerns about ?rearm presence that a tradi-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=190) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
tional dealer or pawn shop might. Further research is needed on this issue. The
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=202) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
relationship between gun shops and crime appears to be entirely local. The effect
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=214) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
of metropolitan-level gun shop presence on local crime rates is insigni?cant. This
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=226) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
is consistent with our theory. We argue that gun shops foster crime directly by
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=238) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
providing opportunities for crime and indirectly most likely by changing percep-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=250) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tions of the neighborhood and instilling fear in residents that weakens social
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=262) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
control efforts. The direct effect should be highly localized—if institutions are at-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=274) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tracting crime because they provide a suitable target, the effect should not spread
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=286) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
far beyond those targets. Likewise, one would not expect the effect of changed
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=298) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
perceptions to spread far beyond the shop; a far-?ung dealer should not change
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=310) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
people’s perceptions of the local neighborhood. Additionally, residents are
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=322) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
unlikely to know how many shops exist in the metro, so metro-level shops
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=334) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
should not affect their perceptions of gun presence. This ?nding also provides
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=346) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
additional support for our theoretical claims. Existing research has shown that
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=358) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
gun shop presence is a viable indicator of ?rearm prevalence at higher levels of
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=370) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
aggregation, like urban counties (Wiebe et al. 2009). Given these ?ndings, me-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=382) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tros with more gun shops should have higher levels of ?rearm prevalence than
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=394) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
metros with fewer dealers. If crime results from increased gun presence, there
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=406) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
should be higher crime rates in tracts in cities with more gun shops. The fact that
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=418) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
we did not ?nd an association between local crime and metropolitan dealers sug-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=430) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
gests that crime is not resulting from guns themselves, but from a process speci?c
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=442) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
to the shop, like the direct and indirect effects proposed above.
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=454) width=265.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Despite our strong, signi?cant ?ndings, there are some limitations to this anal-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=22 y=466) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ysis. First, we focus solely on urban areas, which limits, to a certain extent, how
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=478) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
broadly these ?ndings can be applied. This is not necessarily a shortcoming. Our
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=490) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
hypothesized effects are most likely to occur in urban areas, given location-
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=502) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
speci?c differences in opinions about ?rearms. Urban residents are not only less
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=514) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
likely to own guns than suburban and rural residents, but are also more likely to
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=526) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
support gun control and to hold negative views about ?rearms in general
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=538) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=8
(Blocher 2013). Residents with such views are likely to be negatively in?uenced
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=550) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
by gun shop presence. In this case, context matters. Individuals assess their risk
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=562) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
of ?rearm violence based on culture and the everyday risks that they choose to
page=29 block=246564962 (x=11 y=574) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Page: 30 left:1 upper:1
GunShops and Neighborhood Violent Crime 293
page=30 block=2006384644 (x=195 y=1) width=157.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=HOMJHO+AdvOTb4959fff wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
acknowledge (Braman and Kahan 2003). A rural farmer who uses a ?rearm to
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=34) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
hunt will view ?rearm retailers differently than an urbanite who fears gun vio-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=46) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
lence and supports gun control (Kleck, Gertz, and Bratton 2009). Our theoreti-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=58) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
cal argument is based on the latter context.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=70) width=182.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
Second, although we argue that gun shops directly in?uence crime by providing
page=30 block=22431842 (x=12 y=82) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
suitable targets and indirectly in?uence crime by signaling disorder and discourag-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=94) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
ing informal social control efforts, we cannot directly test our proposed mechan-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=106) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
isms. This is particularly the case with the indirect effects, which we cannot test
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=118) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
because we do not have measures of social control or neighborhood trust. This is a
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=130) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
common issue confronted by researchers studying social disorganization. A poten-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=142) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tial avenue for future research, then, would be to gather social control or organiza-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=154) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
tional measures in a few cities and then test the relationship again.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=166) width=272.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
To conclude, we offer a few policy considerations that stem from our ?ndings.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=12 y=178) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Given that ?rearm ownership is a constitutional right in the United States, policy
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=190) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
efforts seeking to limit the potential negative effects of gun shops must not hin-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=202) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
der gun rights. FFLs are a crucial component of ?rearm regulation and com-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=214) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
merce in the United States (Braga et al. 2002) and must be allowed to operate so
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=226) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
that legal gun ownership may exist. Any policies to limit crime associated with
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=238) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
FFLs must acknowledge this reality.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=250) width=152.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
With this context in mind, our ?rst policy consideration considers the indirect
page=30 block=22431842 (x=12 y=262) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
effects of gun shops on crime, where perceptions of disorder stymie local social
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=274) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
control efforts. If crime rates are increasing because residents hold negative
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=286) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
views of the gun shop and its effect on the neighborhood, efforts to manage resi-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=298) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
dents’ awareness of these shops could reduce fear and its associated effects.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=310) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
Some municipalities already regulate gun shop appearance. For example, munic-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=322) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ipal codes in Washington, DC, and Chicago make it illegal to display ?rearms in
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=334) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
the windows of a gun shop. While this could reduce awareness of the shop, it
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=346) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
could also create unintended consequences. For instance, if businesses opt to
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=358) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
comply by blacking out windows, it could contribute to disorder and crime by
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=370) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
making the shop appear boarded up or neglected. However, while we do not
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=382) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
know whether such ordinances improve the community or stigmatize gun shops
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=394) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in ways that make their presence more evident, municipality ordinances on gun
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=406) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
dealers in general may be a viable policy instrument for improving neighbor-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=418) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
hood crime rates. Future research needs to assess what dealer regulations exist
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=430) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
and how their enforcement and implementation in?uences the effects of such
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=442) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
policies on neighborhood crime.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=454) width=137.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
Another policy consideration is for cities and states to withhold licenses for
page=30 block=22431842 (x=12 y=466) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
new gun shops seeking to open in fragile neighborhoods with a greater risk of
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=478) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
increased crime. Gun shops are not uniformly dispersed across the metro area.
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=490) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
In the cities in our analysis, there were very few gun shops in the richest or the
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=502) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
poorest neighborhoods. Exposure to gun shops was highest in tracts with aver-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=514) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
age levels of disadvantage, and on average, the tracts with no gun shops did not
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=526) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
have higher levels of residential instability than neighborhoods with gun shops
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=538) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
(table 1). Because of this, many of the poorest neighborhoods, which are the
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=550) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
most susceptible to crime, were not affected by gun shops. However, other disor-
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=562) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
ganized neighborhoods, which are also vulnerable to decline and crime
page=30 block=22431842 (x=1 y=574) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=9

page=31 block=5046276 (x=1 y=1) width=77.000000 height=4.000000 fontname=HOMJHO+AdvOTb4959fff wordint.min=1 wordint.max=4
increases, were. Adding gun shops to these neighborhoods could interrupt social
page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=34) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5

page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=58) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
in these fragile neighborhoods.

We close with this ?nal comment. Leaving gun shop interventions to the
page=31 block=246551498 (x=22 y=82) width=331.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7

hoods, making it more likely that already-organized (and thus likely lower-
page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=106) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=7
crime) neighborhoods will be successful, while disorganized neighborhoods will
page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=118) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=5
fail and become even more disorganized. So, regardless of the policy strategy

pursued, efforts at limiting the effects of gun shop presence on neighborhood
page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=142) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
crime that come from outside the neighborhood (i.e., government action) are
page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=154) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
those with the greatest potential effect. Further research is needed to determine
page=31 block=246551498 (x=11 y=166) width=342.000000 height=5.000000 fontname=HOMJGN+AdvOTbc475f09 wordint.min=1 wordint.max=6
policy interventions that are both feasible and effective.

(ICC) of .127, suggesting that approximately 13 percent of the variation in neighbor-

.019, suggesting that approximately 2 percent of the variation in neighborhood rob-

Data for some tracts was collected between 2005 and 2008, and for others, between
